# R13: the relaxed history input remains open; two exact restrictions on its closure

**All new mathematics in this packet is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy. No all-depth history certificate, actual counterexample to that input, or original-target resolution is claimed.**

Grant `1a5403f680a965c1`: one native analytical invocation, two substantive approaches, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers zero, formal submissions zero, additional agents/models zero. The parent owns ledgers and shared administration. This packet contains manual derivations and text/hash operations only.

The conditional primitive--dominant joining calculation passes the algebraic checks below. The missing all-depth history inequality is not obtained. Two conclusions do narrow its continuation:

1. An actual asymmetric-Huber history rejects every proposed history composition that retains the *weakened* left residual with coefficient at least one and then adds only nonnegative residuals, original interpolation rows, and a PSD remainder. This excludes the full non-diagonal bridge/right-row family derived below, not just its diagonal specialization. The history input itself remains positive on this witness.
2. For every fixed finite internal depth, the requested weakened history inequality holds for all sufficiently large enclosing parameters. This is a dimension-uniform, nonnumerical consequence of strict coercivity at the limiting Silver word. Hence an actual failure along enclosing parameters tending to infinity must have internal depth tending to infinity. In particular, a fixed-depth negative-history trajectory that then stalls at a minimizer cannot furnish the requested obstruction. A separate exact argument excludes the proposed farther-minimizer stall mechanism for all cone-projection histories at every physical prefix.

## 1. Scope, parameters, and evidence

The original target is the exact comparison `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1`, or a rigorous negative resolution on the original full function class. Keep `W = 2R`, all integer splits in the benchmark `B_n`, every finite dimension, every original selected minimizer and radius, and the actual last iterate. The accepted benchmark comparison reported in the supplied handoff does not supply the missing upper certificate.

Fix one enclosing integer `m >= 1536` and set

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\quad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

Every child retains these same parameters. Write `N_j = 2^j - 1`, `S_j = sum H_j`, and `C_j = 1 + S_j`. For a word of mass `s`, endpoint `v`, and endpoint gradient `p`, put

\[
E_\Gamma
=2\sum_i h_i(f_i-f_v)
-\sum_i h_i(h_i-1)\|g_i\|^2-\Gamma\|p\|^2.
\tag{1.1}
\]

The exact requested coefficient is

\[
\Gamma_j=\vartheta C_j^2-C_j,\qquad \vartheta=7/10.
\tag{1.2}
\]

Use the nonnegative original interpolation rows

\[
Q_{ij}=2f_i-2f_j-2\langle g_j,x_i-x_j\rangle-\|g_i-g_j\|^2.
\tag{1.3}
\]

These inequalities are necessary for every convex 1-smooth history. The arguments below do not invoke sufficiency of a numerical interpolation system. When an original minimizer is translated to zero, use

\[
Q_{\star i}=-2f_i+2\langle g_i,x_i\rangle-\|g_i\|^2.
\]

Telescoping physical squared distances gives the exact full-class identity

\[
E_\Gamma=\|x_{\rm start}\|^2-\sum_i h_iQ_{\star i}
-2s f_v-\|v\|^2-\Gamma\|p\|^2.
\tag{1.4}
\]

Input hashes checked exactly:

- TASK: `9ef075dfc458d2526b56399ec10c611bdf2932cd8c1a0e35efbf1f41b2bde08b`.
- Parent relaxed PD note: `f7a80cbc05ee2da4b168f61fbc307817550865fa94978fcbfe067f1dae125c09`.

Complete designated source reads include OPERATOR, PROBLEM, R12_FINAL_HANDOFF, R12_RELAXED_PD_JOIN_LEAD, the R11 two-input proof (in particular Sections 3--4), the old dense-history report and root derivation, and the R11 variable-interval note (in particular its conic-hull exclusion). The R7 and relevant R11 shared handoffs were also read to distinguish older failed assertions from the present input. The memory registry was used only for the evidence/authorization boundary; current files and the fresh grant control this invocation.

## 2. Check of the conditional PD join

Let the left physical mass be `s`, the bridge be `b = 1 + k`, its query be `v` with gradient `p`, and the right start be `w = v - bp`. Let the right dominance vector have total `B_R` and weighted gradient `U`. Define its true retained residual by

\[
D_R=\|w\|^2-\sum_R u_iQ_{\star i}-2B_R f_y-\|w-U\|^2.
\]

The original-row identity

\[
Q_{vi}=Q_{\star i}+2f_v-\|p\|^2+2\langle g_i,p-v\rangle
\]

gives, with `A_Q = sum_R u_i Q_vi`,

\[
2B_R(f_v-f_y)=B_R\|p\|^2+2k\langle p,U\rangle+\|U\|^2+D_R+A_Q.
\tag{2.1}
\]

For the parent vector `(h_left, gamma, tau*u_right)`, direct expansion of its dominance residual first gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
D_P={}&E_L+\tau D_R+2(s+\gamma)(f_v-f_y)
+(\Gamma+\gamma-\gamma^2)\|p\|^2\\
&+2\tau(b-\gamma)\langle p,U\rangle-\tau(\tau-1)\|U\|^2.
\end{aligned}
\]

Substituting (2.1) produces the stated parent formula, with

\[
A_q=\Gamma+s+2\gamma-\gamma^2,\quad
B_q=(s+\gamma)k/B_R+\tau(b-\gamma),\quad
C_q=(s+\gamma)/B_R-\tau(\tau-1).
\tag{2.2}
\]

For `C = 1+s`, `r = k/C`, `gamma = 1+3C/4`, `B_R = 1+(32/17)s`, and the prescribed parent total, set

\[
D_0=4(32-15/C),\qquad N=9+128r.
\]

The reductions in the parent note check:

\[
\tau=1+N/D_0,\quad A_q/C^2\ge11/80,
\quad C_q=(119-N)/D_0-N^2/D_0^2,
\]
\[
B_q/C=r-3/4+(128r^2+32r-27/4)/D_0.
\]

The actual ranges `C > 7/3`, `2/5 < r < 5/12` give the parent bounds `C_q > 9/50` and `|B_q| < C/7`, hence determinant greater than `851 C^2/196000`. These checks retain the actual child dominance residual and actual bridge/right rows. No higher primitive positivity was substituted.

The one-step identity also checks by expansion:

\[
E_{h(h+1)}=(h+1)Q_{01}+Q_{10}+(2-h^2)\|g_0-g_1\|^2.
\tag{2.3}
\]

The actual leaf has `1 < h < sqrt(2)`. Both requested leaf inputs follow with the exact differences stated in the parent note. This establishes only the conditional interface; it does not establish any non-leaf instance of (1.2).

## 3. Approach 1: a full non-diagonal original-row join and its actual obstruction

### 3.1 General retained identity

Consider equal child mass `s`, set

\[
C=1+s,\quad b=1+k,\quad T=C+k,\quad C_P=2C+k.
\]

The physical path is `x -> v -> w -> y`, with bridge `w = v-bp`, `p = g_v`, `q = g_y`, and right displacement

\[
G=w-y=\sum_R h_i g_i.
\]

Give both children the same coefficient `Gamma`, and give the parent `Gamma_P`. The elementary history split is

\[
E_P=E_L+E_R+2T(f_v-f_y)
+(\Gamma-bk)\|p\|^2+(\Gamma-\Gamma_P)\|q\|^2.
\tag{3.1}
\]

Keep the actual nonnegative sums

\[
A_v=\sum_R h_iQ_{vi},\qquad A_y=\sum_R h_iQ_{yi}.
\]

Squared-distance telescoping on the right child, with no sign assumption on its residual, gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
A_v={}&2s(f_v-f_y)-E_R-\Gamma\|q\|^2
-\|G\|^2-2k\langle p,G\rangle-s\|p\|^2,\\
A_y={}&-E_R+\|G\|^2+2\langle q,G\rangle-(s+\Gamma)\|q\|^2.
\end{aligned}
\tag{3.2}
\]

The endpoint rows are

\[
Q_{vy}=2(f_v-f_y)-\|p\|^2-2k\langle p,q\rangle-2\langle q,G\rangle-\|q\|^2,
\]
\[
Q_{yv}=-2(f_v-f_y)+(1+2k)\|p\|^2+2\langle p,G\rangle+2\langle p,q\rangle-\|q\|^2.
\tag{3.3}
\]

Choose nonnegative `lambda, mu, t, u` satisfying

\[
t-u=T-\lambda s,\qquad B=1+\lambda+\mu.
\]

Then the exact original-row representation is

\[
E_P=E_L+B E_R+\lambda A_v+\mu A_y+tQ_{vy}+uQ_{yv}
+\mathcal R(p,q,G),
\tag{3.4}
\]

where the symmetric coefficient matrix of the full quadratic remainder has entries

\[
\begin{aligned}
R_{pp}&=\Gamma+C-k^2-2ku,\\
R_{qq}&=\Gamma-\Gamma_P+\lambda\Gamma+\mu(s+\Gamma)+t+u,\\
R_{GG}&=\lambda-\mu,\\
R_{pq}&=tk-u,\quad R_{pG}=\lambda k-u,\quad R_{qG}=t-\mu.
\end{aligned}
\tag{3.5}
\]

The off-diagonal entries multiply twice the corresponding inner products. Thus this attempt genuinely retains a non-diagonal remainder and the right child's signed history. It is not the excluded positive interval/diagonal-slack construction.

Setting

\[
\lambda=\mu=\ell=\frac{C+k}{C-k},\qquad t=\ell,\quad u=k\ell,
\quad B=1+2\ell
\]

eliminates the displacement variable and mixed coefficients. If `J = Gamma+C` and `J_P = Gamma_P+C_P`, the resulting exact identity is

\[
E_P=E_L+B E_R+\ell(A_v+A_y+Q_{vy}+kQ_{yv})
+(J-Bk^2)\|p\|^2+(BJ-J_P)\|q\|^2.
\tag{3.6}
\]

For the standard coefficients `J=C^2`, `J_P=C_P^2`, this becomes

\[
E_P^{\rm std}=E_L^{\rm std}+B E_R^{\rm std}
+\ell(A_v+A_y+Q_{vy}+kQ_{yv})
+\Delta(\|q\|^2-\|p\|^2),
\tag{3.7}
\]
\[
\Delta=Bk^2-C^2
=C^2\frac{(1+r)(r^2+2r-1)}{1-r},\qquad r=k/C.
\]

This matches the signed-defect identity sent independently by the parent. In particular, its positive defect at super-Silver ratios cannot be discarded, and its right history residual cannot be assumed nonnegative at the perturbed standard coefficient.

### 3.2 A literal same-enclosing-parameter trajectory

For any actual child depth `j >= 3`, write `C = C_j`, `k = a c^j`, and `r = k/C`. The exact capacity formula is

\[
C_j=\frac{a}{c-2}c^j+\left(1-\frac{a}{c-2}\right)2^j.
\]

Consequently

\[
r=\frac{c-2}{1+\zeta(2/c)^j},\quad
\zeta=\frac{(5+2\sqrt2)\epsilon+(1+\sqrt2)^2\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon},
\quad 0<\zeta<9\epsilon.
\]

With `c_0 = 1+sqrt(2)` and `r_0 = 1/c_0`, for `j >= 3`,

\[
r_0\zeta(2/c)^j<72\epsilon/c_0^4<c_0\epsilon,
\]

because `c_0^5 = 41+29sqrt(2) > 72`. As `c-2 = r_0+c_0 epsilon`, this proves

\[
r>r_0,\qquad r(2+r)>1.
\tag{3.8}
\]

Use the single static scalar function

\[
f_r(z)=
\begin{cases}
-rz-r^2/2,&z\le-r,\\
z^2/2,&-r\le z\le1,\\
z-1/2,&z\ge1.
\end{cases}
\tag{3.9}
\]

It is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and has unique minimizer zero. Its gradient is `clamp(z,-r,1)`. Take the original start `x_0=C`, so the original radius is exactly `C`.

For a left physical prefix of mass `T_i`, its query is `C-T_i >= 1`, with gradient one. The left child ends at `v=p=1`. The physical bridge sends this to `w=-k`. For the corresponding right prefix, its query is

\[
-k+rT_i\le-r,
\]

with gradient `-r`, and the right child ends exactly at `y=q=-r`, because `k=Cr`. These formulas cover every physical query of the literal word `H_(j+1)`, with no child reset or fictitious sample. Values are given by (3.9). Every minimizer row `Q_star,i` vanishes.

For the requested weakened coefficient on each child, put `delta=1-vartheta=3/10`. The exact residuals are

\[
E_L=\delta C^2,\qquad E_R=\delta C^2r^2,
\]
\[
E_P=C^2\{1-\vartheta[r(2+r)]^2\}.
\tag{3.10}
\]

All rows `Q_vi`, `Q_yi` for right queries, and both endpoint rows `Q_vy,Q_yv`, vanish on this history. More importantly,

\[
E_P-E_L=\vartheta C^2\{1-[r(2+r)]^2\}<0.
\tag{3.11}
\]

Thus no identity or universal inequality of the following form can hold with all displayed terms nonnegative:

\[
E_P\ \ge\ \alpha E_L+\beta_R E_R
+\sum_{i,l}\lambda_{il}Q_{il}+\mathcal P,
\quad \alpha\ge1,\ \beta_R\ge0,\ \lambda_{il}\ge0,\ \mathcal P\ge0.
\tag{3.12}
\]

Here the interpolation rows may be *any* original rows, and `P` may be any PSD quadratic remainder on all original gradient variables. The contradiction is the actual inequality `E_P < E_L`; it does not depend on freeing matrix variables or on a negative eigenvalue of a chosen ansatz. In particular, every choice in (3.4)--(3.5) fails as a nonnegative certificate for this same weakened-coefficient induction.

This exclusion is available at every enclosing `m >= 1536`: take `j=3` to test `H_4`, or `j=m-2` to obtain an unbounded inherited family inside the required range `j+1<m`. It does not exclude a direct whole-word certificate, a genuinely different simultaneous invariant, a coefficient below one on the weak left residual, a stronger left input, or an identity retaining signed quantitative surplus.

### 3.3 The witness does not refute the requested input

The actual ratio satisfies `r < 5/12`. Therefore

\[
\frac{E_P}{C^2}
>1-\frac7{10}\left(\frac{145}{144}\right)^2
=\frac{60185}{207360}>\frac{29}{100}.
\tag{3.13}
\]

Both children and the parent have strictly positive weakened residuals. The obstruction is to paying for this weak parent with the weak child quantities in (3.12). At the limiting Silver ratio, `E_P=E_L` and `E_R=delta C^2 r_0^2>0`; even there, retaining the left residual in full leaves no room for a positive multiple of the weak right residual on this equality history. At actual inherited ratios, (3.11) is strict.

The fixed three-tenths terminal margin is not defeated by the known saturated-root mechanism. This calculation records that fact quantitatively rather than calling the trajectory an input counterexample.

## 4. Approach 2: actual counterexample/stall analysis

The parent's suggested decisive negative construction was an actual initial `H_j` that reaches another minimizer, has zero terminal gradient, and leaves a negative history residual. Later steps would then stall, extending the same negative residual to arbitrarily deep complete words. The following results delimit that mechanism. No such counterexample is constructed.

### 4.1 Strict fixed-depth coercivity at the limiting Silver word

For this subsection only, let `epsilon=0`, so

\[
c_0=1+\sqrt2,\quad a_0=1/c_0,\quad
C_j^0=c_0^j,\quad k_j^0=a_0 C_j^0.
\]

This is a limiting word used in a continuity argument, not a replacement for the actual inherited parameters.

At these parameters `Delta=0` in (3.7). The leaf identity (2.3), with `h=sqrt(2)`, and induction through (3.7) give

\[
E_j^{\rm std}\ge0,\qquad
\Gamma_j^{\rm std}=(C_j^0)^2-C_j^0,
\tag{4.1}
\]

for every physical interpolation-feasible dataset. The proof uses only nonnegative original rows. It proves (4.1) at the exact limiting word and makes no assertion of higher standard primitive positivity at positive epsilon.

We need the equality statement, not merely (4.1):

> If `E_j^std = 0` and the terminal gradient is zero, then every gradient in that word is zero.

For the leaf, equality in (2.3) gives `Q_01=Q_10=0`. With `g_1=0`, their sum is

\[
Q_{01}+Q_{10}=2(h-1)\|g_0\|^2,
\]

so `g_0=0`. For a parent, equality in (3.7) gives zero child residuals and zero endpoint rows, since all their coefficients are positive. The right child, whose terminal gradient is zero, has every gradient zero by induction. Thus `G=0` and `w=y`. The bridge/terminal row sum then is

\[
Q_{vy}+Q_{yv}=2k\|p\|^2.
\]

It must vanish, hence `p=0`. The left induction finishes the proof. This argument uses just the original interpolation inequalities and physical displacement identities, so it also applies to a feasible finite Gram representation without first constructing a realizing function.

The weakened residual at the limiting word satisfies

\[
E_j^{\rm weak}=E_j^{\rm std}+\frac3{10}(C_j^0)^2\|g_{N_j}\|^2.
\tag{4.2}
\]

Consequently it can vanish on interpolation-feasible data only when *all* gradients vanish.

Here is a precise dimension-uniform coercivity consequence. Fix `j`, put `n=N_j`, normalize

\[
\sum_{i=0}^n\|g_i\|^2=1,
\]

and represent the gradients by their `(n+1)`-square Gram matrix `M`, with `M PSD` and `trace M=1`. Translate the terminal position and value to zero in the interpolation system. Physical positions are then determined by

\[
x_i-x_n=\sum_{t=i}^{n-1}h_t g_t.
\]

The two endpoint rows imply

\[
\langle g_n,x_i-x_n\rangle+\tfrac12\|g_i-g_n\|^2
\le f_i-f_n
\le\langle g_i,x_i-x_n\rangle-\tfrac12\|g_i-g_n\|^2.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Since each gradient norm is at most one, these function differences have absolute value at most the fixed word mass `S_j^0`. Hence the set of such normalized feasible Gram/value data is compact: PSD trace-one matrices form a compact set, the values are bounded, and all interpolation constraints are closed. It is nonempty, for example from a nonconstant quadratic history followed by normalization.

The continuous functional (4.2) has strictly positive value everywhere on this compact set, by the equality argument and `trace M=1`. It therefore has a positive minimum `eta_j`. Homogeneity gives

\[
E_j^{\rm weak}\ge\eta_j\sum_{i=0}^{N_j}\|g_i\|^2
\qquad(\epsilon=0),\qquad \eta_j>0.
\tag{4.4}
\]

No numerical optimization, solver, or finite-array calculation is hidden in this compactness proof. The finite Gram size removes dependence on the original ambient dimension.

### 4.2 Transfer to actual enclosing parameters; no fixed-depth obstruction

For each fixed `j`, the actual coefficients `H_j(m)`, `S_j(m)`, and `Gamma_j(m)` converge to their limiting values as `m -> infinity`. Suppose there were a sequence of enclosing parameters tending to infinity and actual histories at this fixed depth with

\[
E_j^{\rm weak}<\tfrac12\eta_j\sum_i\|g_i\|^2.
\]

A nonzero violating history can be normalized to gradient trace one. The physical masses remain uniformly bounded because `j` is fixed; (4.3) therefore gives a uniform value bound. Extract a convergent subsequence of the finite Gram matrices and values. The interpolation constraints and the history functional are continuous in these data and in the physical step coefficients. The limit is an interpolation-feasible normalized dataset for the exact Silver word with residual at most `eta_j/2`, contradicting (4.4).

Thus the following statement is established in this manual draft:

\[
\boxed{\text{For every fixed }j\ge1\text{, there is }M_j<\infty
\text{ such that for all }m\ge M_j,
\quad E_j\ge\tfrac12\eta_j\sum_i\|g_i\|^2.}
\tag{4.5}
\]

It holds for all finite dimensions and all original convex 1-smooth histories, with the actual same-enclosing-m coefficients. A finite collection of depths can be handled by taking the maximum of the corresponding thresholds. Therefore, if the requested all-depth input fails along a sequence `m -> infinity`, its violating depth must tend to infinity along a subsequence; bounded-depth failures are impossible eventually.

This also excludes the parent's fixed-depth absorbing-prefix mechanism: on a history ending at a minimizer the terminal gradient penalty is zero, and a negative history residual contradicts (4.5). For a projection body whose minimizer rows vanish, the special proposed farther-minimizer condition would be exactly such a negative residual.

The order of quantifiers matters. We have `for every fixed j, eventually in m`, not `eventually in m, for every j<m`. No bound on the growth of `M_j` or decay of `eta_j` is supplied. Equation (4.5) is a proved restriction on possible counterexamples, not the task's all-depth input or a substitute for it.

### 4.3 A separate all-prefix exclusion for cone projections

There is also an exact exclusion of farther-minimizer absorption for the whole cone-projection subclass, at arbitrary inherited depth. Let `K` be a closed convex cone and

\[
f(x)=\tfrac12\|P_Kx\|^2,
\qquad \arg\min f=K^\circ.
\]

Write `g=P_Kx`, `n=x-g`. The projection optimality conditions for a cone give `n in K^polar`, `<n,g>=0`, and, for any vector `z`, `<z,P_Kz>=||P_Kz||^2`. These follow by testing the projection condition against zero and positive multiples of the projected vector.

For `h>1` and `r=h-1`, the next gradient `g^+=P_K(n-rg)` obeys

\[
\|g^+\|^2=\langle g^+,n-rg\rangle
\le-r\langle g^+,g\rangle\le r\|g^+\|\|g\|.
\]

Hence `||g^+|| <= r||g||`. For any originally selected minimizer `z_star in K^polar`,

\[
\|x-z_\star\|^2-\|x-hg-z_\star\|^2
\ge h(2-h)\|g\|^2.
\tag{4.6}
\]

Define physical prefix products `P_0=1`, `P_t=prod_(i<t)(h_i-1)`. Every prefix of every actual inherited `H_j` satisfies `P_t <= 1`. To prove this, the total product is

\[
P(H_j)=\beta^{2^j-1}c^{-j},
\tag{4.7}
\]

by induction on the concatenation. Prefixes in the left child obey the claim inductively. After that child and its bridge, the product is

\[
P(H_j)\,a c^j=a\beta^{2^j-1}<1,
\]

and multiplication by any right-child prefix again preserves the bound.

Set `v_i=||g_i||^2/P_i^2`. These numbers are nonnegative and nonincreasing. For every physical prefix length `T >= 1`, summation by parts gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i<T}h_i(2-h_i)\|g_i\|^2
={}&(1-P_T^2)v_{T-1}\\
&+\sum_{i=0}^{T-2}(1-P_{i+1}^2)(v_i-v_{i+1})\ge0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{4.8}
\]

Combining (4.6)--(4.8) yields

\[
\boxed{\|x_T-z_\star\|\le\|x_0-z_\star\|
\quad\text{for every physical prefix and every original }z_\star\in K^\circ.}
\tag{4.9}
\]

This is a distance bound from the original start at each prefix; it does not claim the distance decreases at each individual long step. It rules out reaching a more distant selected-minimizer point in this entire subclass, even if the terminal objective is zero. The same conclusion holds for a compact cone truncation whenever its cap is nonbinding on the complete history; its polar minimizer set is unchanged.

The prior R11 cone result concerned objective decay and the closest polar-minimizer radius. Equation (4.9) addresses every originally selected polar minimizer and the proposed stall defect. It does not exclude a bounded origin-vertex body whose cap becomes active, a nonhomogeneous body, or other smooth-convex functions. No general-history inequality follows from it.

# R7 handoff: P_7 is false on an actual asymmetric Huber trajectory

**All new mathematics is UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** One native invocation under allocation `9551ad5108ac3aae`; one substantive proof line used; mathematical programs, arrays, solvers and formal submissions zero. The optional second line was not used. The call is complete and stops here.

## Decisive result and its exact scope

For **every enclosing integer `m>=1536`**, the dispatched all-level terminal-potential assertion fails at **`j=7`**, using the same enclosing parameters at every child. This is an actual one-dimensional globally convex 1-smooth function and a literal 127-step GD trajectory. It is not a formal Gram direction and not merely a failure of one selected dual certificate.

This result **does not reject `P_m` for `m>=1536`**, does not reject every weaker potential, and does not establish or refute the full objective bound `W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m)`. The accepted conditional negative route remains conditional.

## Self-contained counterexample

Fix `epsilon=1/(4m)`, `lambda=1+sqrt(2)`, `beta=1-epsilon`, `c=lambda(1+epsilon)`, `a=beta/c`, and

\[
H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\quad
S_j=\sum H_j,\quad A_j=1+S_j.
\]

Take `A=A_6`, `d=ac^6`, `r=d/A`. Define

\[
f_r(x)=
\begin{cases}
-rx-r^2/2,&x\le-r,\\
x^2/2,&-r\le x\le1,\\
x-1/2,&x\ge1.
\end{cases}
\]

Its derivative is the clamp to `[-r,1]`, hence it is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The unique minimizer is zero. Start at `x_0=A` and run the actual word `H_7=(H_6,1+d,H_6)`.

- In the first copy, partial mass `u` gives `x=A-u>=1` and gradient `1`. Its endpoint is `1`.
- The bridge lands at `-d=-Ar`.
- In the second copy, partial mass `u` gives `x=-r(A-u)<=-r` and gradient `-r`.
- Terminal data are `x_127=-r`, `g_127=-r`, `f_127=r^2/2`, and the original initial squared radius is `A^2`.

The whole mass satisfies `S_7+1=A(2+r)`. For `q_7=(1+epsilon)^(-7)`, the proposed `P_7` left side divided by the initial squared radius is exactly

\[
q_7[r(2+r)]^2+(1-q_7)\frac{r^2(2+r)}A.
\]

The complete proof establishes, with no decimal estimates,

\[
r>\sqrt2-1+\frac43\epsilon,
\qquad
q_7[r(2+r)]^2
>1+\frac7{15}\epsilon(1-112\epsilon)>1.
\]

The second summand is also strictly positive. This is the strict violation.

The exact scalar estimate uses

\[
A_j=vc^j+(1-v)2^j,\qquad v=\frac{1-\epsilon}{c(c-2)},
\]

\[
r=\frac{\sqrt2-1+\lambda\epsilon}{1+\epsilon R(\epsilon)},
\quad
R(\epsilon)=\frac{5+2\sqrt2+\lambda^2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}
\left(\frac2\lambda\right)^6(1+\epsilon)^{-6}<\frac{13}5.
\]

Useful rational checks are `sqrt(2)>140/99`, `sqrt(2)<99/70`, `40*68^3<13*99^3`, and `epsilon<=1/6144`. The full report spells out every inequality and sign.

## What the final extraction does and does not establish

The parent-proposed extraction was checked independently. If a valid terminal inequality has coefficients `2A` on the gap and `B` on the terminal squared gradient, then interpolation and Cauchy--Schwarz imply

\[
W\le\frac2{2A+1+\sqrt{4B+1}}.
\]

For `A=S_m`, `B=q_mS_m(S_m+1)`, one has `q_m>exp(-1/4)>225/289`; an explicit rational exponential-series bound is included in the proof. Therefore a separately valid `P_m` would give the required `17/(16D_m)` upper bound. Only the proposed **uniform all-level route** has been rejected here.

No new composition rule was proved. Zhang--Jiang's retained primitive inequalities and the GSW history characterization were source-checked, but their hypotheses were not dropped or silently extended. The optional idea of an extra finite startup penalty has no established full-class certificate in this call and is not a result or an automatic renewal request.

## Recovery and controls

Read `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md` in this same directory completely before any reuse. It contains the exact original normalization, actual function and trajectory, all parameter estimates, extraction audit, source links and scope restrictions. No finite sampled evidence enters it.

No truth-store or Goal mutation, formal submission, worker/service start, old-budget reopening, publication, finalization, revocation or shutdown occurred. Parent confirmed `proof_lines_used=1`; the invocation has stopped. Any later repair is separate work requiring a fresh bounded allocation under the standing human authorization.

# R7: an actual smooth-convex counterexample to the all-level terminal potential

**Status: all new mathematics below is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy.** This is a complete analytical counterexample to the proposed assertion that every level satisfies `P_j`. It is not a counterexample to the terminal assertion `P_m`, and it does not decide the original minimax problem. No formal submission has been made.

Allocation `9551ad5108ac3aae`: one native analytical invocation; **one substantive proof line used**; mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers and formal submissions: zero. The optional second line is unused. This invocation stops after these artifacts.

## 1. Original target and exact scope of this result

For a predetermined nonnegative word `h` of length `n`, gradient descent is

\[
x_{i+1}=x_i-\frac{h_i}{L}\nabla f(x_i),
\qquad
W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}.
\]

The supremum includes every finite dimension, every convex differentiable function with an `L`-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer, and every distinct starting point. Write `W_n^*=inf_{h>=0} W_n(h)` and `U_n^ZJ=1/B_n`, where `B_n` is the exact original all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang dominant capacity. The original question is whether `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`. The normalization is `W=2R`.

Fix an arbitrary enclosing integer `m>=1536`. Throughout every child uses the same parameters

\[
\lambda=1+\sqrt2,\quad
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,
\quad c=\lambda(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\frac{\beta}{c},
\]

\[
H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),
\qquad S_j=\sum H_j,\quad A_j=1+S_j.
\]

All steps are strictly positive; `H_j` has physical length `2^j-1`. Accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201` states `B_{2^m-1}/(1+2S_m)<47/50`. It conditionally reduces a negative answer to the original question to a full-function upper bound

\[
W_{2^m-1}(H_m)\le\frac{17}{16(1+2S_m)}.
\]

That full-function bound remains unproved.

The R7 candidate was the following universal inequality, with `L=1`:

\[
\|x_{\rm end}-x_\star\|^2
+2S_j(f_{\rm end}-f_\star)
+q_jS_j(S_j+1)\|g_{\rm end}\|^2
\le \|x_{\rm start}-x_\star\|^2,
\qquad q_j=(1+\epsilon)^{-j}. \tag{P_j}
\]

**Draft conclusion. For every enclosing integer `m>=1536`, there is an explicit one-dimensional admissible function and an actual trajectory of the inherited word `H_7` that strictly violates `P_7`.** The same fixed child level works for all such enclosing `m`. Thus the claimed all-level induction is false. The conclusion does not assert that `P_m` fails when `m>=1536`.

## 2. One explicit construction for any symmetric concatenation

Let `h` be any finite strictly positive word of mass `S`, put `A=S+1`, and choose any `d>0`. Consider the literal concatenation `(h,1+d,h)`. Set

\[
r=\frac dA>0.
\]

Define a single global function on the real line by

\[
f_r(x)=
\begin{cases}
-rx-\tfrac12r^2,&x\le-r,\\
\tfrac12x^2,&-r\le x\le1,\\
x-\tfrac12,&x\ge1.
\end{cases} \tag{1}
\]

The function and its derivative agree at both junctions. Its derivative is

\[
f_r'(x)=\max\{-r,\min\{x,1\}\}.
\]

This derivative is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. Consequently `f_r` is globally convex, differentiable and 1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is `x_star=0`, with minimum zero. This is an actual function, not proposed interpolation data.

Start at `x_0=A`. In the first copy of `h`, let `u` denote the sum of all steps already taken. Every iterate is

\[
x=A-u\ge1,\qquad g=1,
\qquad 0\le u\le S.
\]

This follows directly by induction using the prescribed GD recursion. The first copy ends at `x=1`. The bridge is then

\[
1-(1+d)f_r'(1)=-d=-Ar.
\]

In the second copy of `h`, the corresponding iterates are

\[
x=-Ar+ur=-r(A-u)\le-r,\qquad g=-r,
\qquad 0\le u\le S.
\]

They again follow by the exact GD recursion. The actual terminal data are therefore

\[
x_N=-r,\qquad g_N=-r,\qquad f_N=\tfrac12r^2,
\qquad \|x_0-x_\star\|^2=A^2. \tag{2}
\]

Every physical step in both copies is retained. No property of the order within `h` was needed except positivity, which ensures every partial sum lies between zero and `S`.

Let the full mass be `T=2S+1+d`, and put `A_+=T+1=2A+d=A(2+r)`. For an arbitrary coefficient `q`, the ratio between the left and right sides of the joint terminal inequality with coefficients `2T` and `qT(T+1)` is exactly

\[
\frac{r^2[1+T+qT(T+1)]}{A^2}
=q\,[r(2+r)]^2
+(1-q)\frac{r^2(2+r)}A. \tag{3}
\]

In particular, if `0<q<1` and `q[r(2+r)]^2>1`, this actual trajectory strictly violates the proposed terminal inequality. This is a necessary test for the universal inequality itself, independent of a chosen dual multiplier support or PSD ansatz.

## 3. Exact inherited-parameter estimate at child level six

We now use `h=H_6`, `A=A_6` and `d=ac^6`. Thus the full word in Section 2 is precisely `H_7` with the original enclosing parameters. Write

\[
s=\sqrt2-1,\qquad r=\frac{ac^6}{A_6}.
\]

The mass recurrence is

\[
A_0=1,\qquad A_{j+1}=2A_j+ac^j.
\]

Its exact solution, verified by substitution and the initial value, is

\[
A_j=vc^j+(1-v)2^j,
\qquad v=\frac a{c-2}=\frac{1-\epsilon}{c(c-2)}.
\]

Here `c>lambda>2`, `lambda(lambda-2)=1`, and `0<1-epsilon<1`, so `0<v<1`. Substitution gives

\[
r=\frac{c-2}{1+\left(\frac1v-1\right)(2/c)^6}
=\frac{s+\lambda\epsilon}{1+\epsilon R(\epsilon)}, \tag{4}
\]

where

\[
R(\epsilon)=
\frac{5+2\sqrt2+\lambda^2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}
\left(\frac2\lambda\right)^6(1+\epsilon)^{-6}.
\tag{5}
\]

For completeness, the coefficient in (5) follows by expanding

\[
c(c-2)-(1-\epsilon)
=(5+2\sqrt2)\epsilon+\lambda^2\epsilon^2.
\]

We will prove the convenient exact estimate

\[
R(\epsilon)<\frac{13}5,
\qquad
r>s+\frac43\epsilon
\quad\text{for }0<\epsilon\le\frac1{6144}. \tag{6}
\]

First, `sqrt(2)<10/7` and `lambda^2<6`, hence

\[
\frac{5+2\sqrt2+\lambda^2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}
<\frac{55/7+6\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}<8.
\]

The last inequality is equivalent to `14 epsilon<1/7`, which follows from `epsilon<=1/6144`. Also `sqrt(2)>140/99`, since `140^2=19600<19602=2*99^2`. Thus

\[
\left(\frac2\lambda\right)^2=12-8\sqrt2<\frac{68}{99}.
\]

The exact integer comparison

\[
40\cdot68^3=12577280<12613887=13\cdot99^3
\]

implies `(68/99)^3<13/40`. Dropping the factor `(1+epsilon)^(-6)<1` from (5) now proves

\[
R(\epsilon)<8\left(\frac{68}{99}\right)^3<\frac{13}5.
\]

Subtracting `s` from (4), and using this bound with positive numerator and denominator, gives

\[
\frac{r-s}{\epsilon}
=\frac{\lambda-sR(\epsilon)}{1+\epsilon R(\epsilon)}
>\frac{\lambda-(13/5)s}{1+(13/5)\epsilon}.
\tag{7}
\]

Since `99^2=9801>9800=2*70^2`, we have `s<29/70`. Consequently

\[
\lambda-\frac{13}5s=2-\frac85s>\frac{234}{175}.
\]

Finally,

\[
\frac{234}{175}-\frac43=\frac2{525}
>\frac{13}{23040}
=\frac43\frac{13}{5\cdot6144}.
\]

Together with (7) and `epsilon<=1/6144`, this proves the second part of (6). All estimates use the enclosing `m` only through its exact `epsilon=1/(4m)`.

## 4. Strict violation of the proposed P_7

The identity `s(2+s)=1` gives, with `delta=r-s>4 epsilon/3`,

\[
r(2+r)=1+2\sqrt2\,\delta+\delta^2
>1+\frac{8\sqrt2}{3}\epsilon.
\]

Squaring the positive sides and using `sqrt(2)>7/5` yields

\[
[r(2+r)]^2
>1+\frac{16\sqrt2}{3}\epsilon
>1+\frac{112}{15}\epsilon. \tag{8}
\]

The strictly convex function `z -> (1+z)^(-7)` lies above its tangent at zero when `z>0`. Therefore

\[
q_7=(1+\epsilon)^{-7}>1-7\epsilon>0.
\]

Multiplying this with (8), whose right-hand side is positive, gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
q_7[r(2+r)]^2
&>(1-7\epsilon)\left(1+\frac{112}{15}\epsilon\right)\\
&=1+\frac7{15}\epsilon(1-112\epsilon)>1.
\end{aligned} \tag{9}
\]

The last inequality holds because `epsilon<=1/6144<1/112`. Since `0<q_7<1`, the other summand in (3) is strictly positive. Thus the actual function (1) and trajectory (2), with the inherited `H_7`, satisfy

\[
\frac{\|x_{127}\|^2+2S_7 f_r(x_{127})
+q_7S_7(S_7+1)|f_r'(x_{127})|^2}{|x_0|^2}
>1+\frac7{15}\epsilon(1-112\epsilon)>1.
\tag{10}
\]

This is the advertised counterexample for every integer `m>=1536`. The horizon `127=2^7-1` is the literal length of the counterexample word, and `7<=m`. No finite sampling or limiting approximation enters the proof. A counterexample at `L=1` suffices to reject the universal assertion; multiplying (1) by any `L>0` gives the corresponding scaled example with the same normalized GD trajectory.

## 5. Audit of the proposed final extraction

This audit is part of evaluating the same proposed proof line. It does not furnish the missing terminal inequality.

Suppose, for an actual terminal pair `(z,g)` relative to a minimizer and a nonnegative gap `F`, one has an established inequality

\[
\|z\|^2+2AF+B\|g\|^2\le R_0^2,
\qquad A,B\ge0.
\]

Smooth-convex interpolation gives `F<=<g,z>-||g||^2/2`. If `g=0`, then `F=0`. Otherwise, writing `t=||g||>0`, Cauchy--Schwarz gives `||z||>=F/t+t/2`. Hence

\[
\begin{aligned}
R_0^2
&\ge (F/t+t/2)^2+2AF+Bt^2\\
&=(2A+1)F+F^2/t^2+(B+1/4)t^2\\
&\ge [2A+1+\sqrt{4B+1}]F.
\end{aligned}
\]

The last step is the elementary inequality `u^2+v^2>=2uv`. It follows that

\[
W\le\frac2{2A+1+\sqrt{4B+1}}. \tag{11}
\]

For the proposed terminal coefficient `B=q_m S_m(S_m+1)`, set `D_m=2S_m+1`. Then

\[
1+4B=q_mD_m^2+1-q_m\ge q_mD_m^2.
\]

The asserted rational comparison on `q_m` is also sound. Since `log(1+u)<u` for `u>0`, `q_m>exp(-1/4)`. To check the rational threshold without decimal estimates, the exponential series at `1/4` satisfies

\[
\begin{aligned}
e^{1/4}
&<1+\frac14+\frac1{32}+\frac1{384}
+\frac{1/6144}{1-1/20}\\
&=\frac{37473}{29184}<\frac{289}{225}.
\end{aligned}
\]

For the series bound, writing `t_k=(1/4)^k/k!`, one has `t_(k+1)/t_k=1/[4(k+1)]<=1/20` for every `k>=4`, with strict inequality after `k=4`. The last comparison follows from

\[
37473\cdot225=8431425<8434176=289\cdot29184.
\]

Thus `q_m>225/289=(15/17)^2`. If `P_m` were independently proved, (11) would indeed yield `W<=17/(16D_m)` and the accepted comparison would close the negative route. The problem with the dispatched candidate is its universal all-level premise, not this extraction or its normalization.

## 6. Source checks, scope and stopping record

Read in full: current `OPERATOR.md`, the complete original `PROBLEM.md`, this allocation's `TASK.md`, `R6_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`, `R5_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261008.md`, and the statement and proof of accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201`.

The [Zhang--Jiang primary source, Sections 2.2, 3.1 and 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2) was checked. Its primitive inequality retains weighted interpolation terms against the minimizer; the displayed terminal potential follows by dropping those terms. The concatenation proof uses the retained inequality and does not license arbitrary weakened endpoint composition. The [GSW Proposition 6 and appendix proof](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.16249v2) and the [terminal-certificate source, Definition 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v2) were also checked. No new composition theorem, dual equivalence or multiplier restriction is imported into the counterexample above. Its proof uses only the explicit one-dimensional function and the literal GD trajectory.

The public R5/R6 scope records were respected: no private worker memory, raw mathematical arrays or previous numerical budgets were opened. A quick memory-registry check was used only for target and evidence-scope consistency; current task and source files control this report.

The outcome is not an obstruction limited to the old fixed-source terminal-fan family: equation (10) contradicts the endpoint inequality itself on an admissible function. Conversely, equation (10) says nothing about the complete `H_m` run for `m>=1536`, about an alternative coefficient sequence, or about the full objective upper bound. A fixed startup reduction of the gradient coefficient could remove this particular objection; no claim of validity for such a repair is made, and no second proof line is spent on an unsupported adjustment.

The original target remains **OPEN**. Parent instruction confirmed closure of this invocation with one substantive line. There were no new agents or models, mathematical programs, solvers, formal requests, service/worker actions, truth-store edits, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal mutations.

## Original target and concrete negative route

The question is lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1, W=2R and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n with the exact original all-integer-split recurrence. For every integer m>=1536, fix eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c, and define H0 empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j) for0<=j<m using these SAME enclosing parameters at every child. Let S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m, n_m=2^m-1. The accepted comparison is B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50. If you prove the FULL FUNCTION-CLASS bound

    W_(n_m)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for EVERY m>=1536,

then B_(n_m)W_(n_m)(H_m)<799/800 for this infinite subsequence, disproving the original ratio-one limit. No finite-m numerical value or restricted-function bound establishes that premise.

## Proposed direct all-level potential -- a conjecture to prove or reject

Set L=1 only for notation. Consider the universal terminal inequality for the ACTUAL H_j trajectory and any minimizer x_star:

    ||x_end-x_star||^2 + 2*S_j*(f_end-f_star)
       + q_j*S_j*(S_j+1)*||g_end||^2 <= ||x_start-x_star||^2,    (P_j)

Here is the elementary final extraction, which you should independently check. Smooth convex interpolation gives f_end-f_star<=<g_end,x_end-x_star>-||g_end||^2/2. If a terminal inequality holds with coefficients2A on f and B on ||g||^2, then eliminating the actual terminal radius/gradient gives

    W<=2/(2A+1+sqrt(4B+1)).

For (P_m), q_m>exp(-1/4)>225/289=(15/17)^2. Therefore sqrt(1+4q_m*S_m*(S_m+1)) >=(15/17)(2S_m+1), and the desired17/(16D_m) follows. The elementary exponential comparison can be proved by a rational series bound; do not use decimal estimates as a certificate. At j=0, (P_j) is exact. At eps=0 this is the familiar joint Silver terminal potential. Its persistence for eps>0 is the substantive unproved part.

Prove (P_j) uniformly through a dimension-free full-function inequality, an exact interpolation certificate with all necessary rows and PSD directions, or a stronger true induction that yields the same final bound. # Handoff: relaxed U with genuine star-source routing

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Allocation `52911a3e705b3954`.
One analytical invocation completed; STOP, no automatic renewal.

## Result

`REPORT.md` contains an independent full derivation of the relaxed original
normal-row coefficient interface, followed by exactly two explicit laws
within one global dense regenerative-routing mechanism. Both fail
analytically as full-space PSD certificates on every actual same-m H_m,
for m>=1536. No actual adverse GD trajectory or upper bound was obtained.

## Full interface to retain

Use `alpha=17/16`, `eta=1/16`, physical labels `0,...,n`, auxiliary
`p=P_D(x0/M)`, and zero star. Original normals are
`Q_ij=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0`. With destination scaling
`mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij`, physical `a_j=1`, `a_p=1/M`, write
`s_j=mu_{*,j}`. Internal out/in exclude star rows. Exact x0 cancellation is

    out_i-in_i-s_i = 2 1_(i=n)-2 alpha 1_(i=p),
    sum_j s_j = 1/8.

The complete gradient polynomial and every matrix coefficient are
REPORT (3.7)--(3.11). At common nonstar gradients its value is
`[-eta M+sum s_j t_j]||v||^2`; it need not vanish. No general clock-contact,
p-absorption, or terminal-zero quotient follows. The exact p-square
completion in (3.13) is a separately derived valid reduction, not an
absorption premise; neither exclusion needs it.

## Both laws and their exact failures

For `S=sum h`, `M=1+2S`, define the physical probability profile

    pi_i=h_i/M (i<n), pi_n=(S+1)/M.

For a specified theta, take `q_i=theta pi_i`, `q_p=1-theta`,
`kappa=33/16`, routing intensity `L=2 kappa/q_p`, and

    r_i=L q_i+kappa 1_(i=n)-kappa 1_(i=p),
    mu_ij=r_i q_j for nonstar i!=j,
    s_n=s_p=1/16, all other star sources zero.

Original lambda equals mu at physical destinations and M mu at p.
All diagonal and destination-star rows are zero. This specifies every
original coefficient, all nonnegative. In particular `r_p=kappa>0`, so
all auxiliary-to-physical rows are positive. The common-gradient value
is `eta(1+S)||v||^2>0`.

1. **Balanced destinations:** `theta=1/2`. Original diagonal
   `P_ii=L q_i[1-q_i-h_i sum_(j>i)q_j]`. For the fixed H3-prefix bridge
   `i=3`, write `C=1+S_2`, `k=beta c`, `h_3=1+k`.
   Negativity at H3 is exactly
   `C(3k-5)+(k-1)(k-2)>0`, since `k>7/3`.
   The sign persists as the enclosing full-word M increases. Thus
   `g_3=v`, all other original gradients including p and g_n zero,
   gives a negative full polynomial for every actual H_m.
2. **Largest-step damped destinations:** `d=max h_i`,
   `theta=1/(2d)`. All individual diagonals are positive. For an index i
   with `h_i=d>=3`, keep `(g_i,g_n)` and set all other original
   coordinates zero. With `y=Lq_n`, the exact 2-by-2 block satisfies
   `D_iD_n<y(y+alpha)` and `C_cross^2/4>=(y+kappa)^2`.
   Since `(y+kappa)^2-y(y+alpha)>0`, its determinant is strictly negative.
   This holds for EVERY intensity `L>=kappa/q_p`; increasing the
   circulating or p-return flow cannot repair this law.

These are full formal quadratic witnesses, not claimed legal projection
histories. The second rule repaired the first rule's diagonal problem but
retained an irreparable mixed-gradient problem within this mechanism.

## Target implication and missing result

REPORT Section 7 derives the exact conditional implication using the full
polar minimum cone C_D. An all-body bound
`U1(T_Hm x)<=17 UM(x)/16` eventually in m would give the original

# Relaxed U: two dense regenerative flow laws with genuine star sources

JOINT UNVERIFIED. One direct analytical invocation, allocation
`52911a3e705b3954`, 2026-10-08. Original minimax target OPEN.

## 1. Outcome and scope

The relaxed coefficient interface was derived independently from the original
normal rows. Its source budget is exactly `1/8`. The complete matrix retains
the physical terminal gradient, the auxiliary gradient, all physical pairs,
and auxiliary-to-physical rows. Neither common-gradient contact nor
absorption nor a terminal-zero quotient is assumed.

Exactly two explicit laws were tested within one mechanism: every nonstar
source routes according to one common destination distribution, with a
circulating flow and two genuine star-source rows. In both laws, the star
sends `1/16` to the physical terminal and `1/16` to the auxiliary point.
Both laws have strictly positive auxiliary-to-physical coefficients and a
strictly positive common-gradient value. Every original coefficient is
specified below and is nonnegative.

- Law 1 assigns half the destination probability to the physical canonical
  mass profile. Its literal `g_3` diagonal is strictly negative on every
  actual `H_m`, for every enclosing `m>=1536`.
- Law 2 reduces the physical destination probability according to the
  largest step. All coordinate diagonals then become positive. Nevertheless,
  its full two-coordinate principal block consisting of the largest-step
  gradient and the terminal gradient has strictly negative determinant.
  This obstruction holds for every admissible amount of circulating flow,
  including amounts larger than the explicit law uses.

These are analytical exclusions of the stated coefficient laws. The formal
negative directions are not asserted to be projection histories. No genuine
GD counterexample, full-class upper bound, or asymptotic separation was
obtained. The all-depth construction is BLOCKED at these two laws.

## 2. Governing inputs and normalization

The current `OPERATOR.md`, original project `PROBLEM.md`, and complete TASK
were read. The operator's current explicit continuation and live-Goal
confirmation supersede the historical pause notes.

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`, Parts A--C, supplies finite-word
projection-simplex universality, the entire polar minimum set, and the
actual same-enclosing-parameter H3 law. Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` supplies
the benchmark ratio. Their relevant statements were read directly; the
actual H3 identity was also read. They do not supply the all-depth bound.

The relaxed-interface lead and the prior projection-flow report were used
as unverified leads/conventions. The original equations below are derived
anew. The complete-graph H3/H4 numerical report was read only as recorded
finite evidence, without loading its arrays or treating its candidates as
premises. The prior joint-reserve report's two failed laws and their scope
were read: neither its absorption-product gluing nor its moment-dual
destination rule is retried here. The present common-destination routing
is global on the original word; it has no inherited child multiplier
array, child reserve, or child moment-matching equation.

Fix one enclosing integer `m>=1536` and use throughout all children

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
 \quad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

For the actual full word let `n=2^m-1`, `S=sum H_m`, and `M=1+2S`.
The target of this invocation is

\[
 U_1(T_{H_m}x)\le\alpha U_M(x),\qquad
 \alpha=17/16,\quad \eta=\alpha-1=1/16.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Here `U_d(x)=max_{g in D}(2<x,g>-d||g||^2)` for every nonempty compact
convex body `D` containing zero, in every finite dimension. Physical GD
uses `P_D` and the original steps. The original error normalization remains
`W=2R`; the certificate polynomial below is unrelated to that error `R`.

## 3. Derivation of the full relaxed interface

The following derivation applies to any finite nonnegative word, with
`S>0`. Define physical labels `0,...,n`, auxiliary label `p`, and star `*`.
Write

\[
 T_i=\sum_{k<i}h_k,\quad t_i=1+T_i,\quad t_p=M,
 \quad G_i=g_i=P_D(x_i),\quad G_p=p=P_D(x_0/M),
\]

\[
 B_iG=g_i+\sum_{k<i}h_kg_k,\qquad B_pG=Mp,
 \qquad X_*=G_*=0.
\tag{3.1}
\]

For physical destinations put `a_j=1`; for destination `p` put `a_p=1/M`.
Then `X_j-G_j=a_j(x_0-B_jG)`. The original projection normals are

\[
 Q_{ij}=\langle X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j\rangle\le0.
\tag{3.2}
\]

For nonnegative original multipliers `lambda_ij`, the sufficient
certificate is

\[
 R_\alpha=J_\alpha+\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{ij}Q_{ij}\succeq0,
 \qquad J_\alpha=\alpha U_M(x_0)-U_1(x_n),
\tag{3.3}
\]

where the actual maximizer expressions replace both U values before
forming a polynomial in the formal coordinates. On an actual history,
`J_alpha>=R_alpha`; hence a globally PSD polynomial proves (2.1).
No completeness or dual-attainment assertion about this certificate cone
is used.

Rows with destination `*`, and diagonal rows, are identically zero and
are assigned coefficient zero. For every other row set
`mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij`. Let `I={0,...,n,p}`,
`s_j=mu_{*,j}`, and define `out_i` and `in_i` using only the internal
edges on I, with the self edges omitted. Expanding the objective gives

\[
 J_\alpha=2\langle x_0,\alpha p-g_n\rangle
  +2\Big\langle g_n,\sum_{k<n}h_kg_k\Big\rangle
  +\|g_n\|^2-\alpha M\|p\|^2.
\tag{3.4}
\]

There is no squared x0 term. Global PSD therefore forces its entire
linear coefficient to vanish. Coordinate by coordinate this is exactly

\[
 \boxed{\operatorname{out}_i-\operatorname{in}_i-s_i
       =2\mathbf1_{i=n}-2\alpha\mathbf1_{i=p},\qquad i\in I.}
\tag{3.5}
\]

Summation gives

\[
 \boxed{\sum_{j\in I}s_j=2(\alpha-1)=1/8.}
\tag{3.6}
\]

Thus full divergence, including the star, is the proposed relaxed
divergence. This derivation does not force any of the `s_j` or any
auxiliary outgoing coefficient to vanish.

After (3.5), the complete polynomial is

\[
 \boxed{R_\alpha=
 2\Big\langle g_n,\sum_{k<n}h_kg_k\Big\rangle+\|g_n\|^2
 -\alpha M\|p\|^2
 +\sum_{i,j\in I}\mu_{ij}\langle B_jG,G_j-G_i\rangle
 +\sum_{j\in I}s_j\langle B_jG,G_j\rangle.}
\tag{3.7}
\]

If `b_j` is the coordinate column of `B_j`, `H=sum h_i e_i`, and
`sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2`, its full symmetric matrix is

\[
 P=2\operatorname{sym}(e_n,H)+e_ne_n^T-\alpha Me_pe_p^T
   +\sum_{i,j\in I}\mu_{ij}\operatorname{sym}(b_j,e_j-e_i)
   +\sum_{j\in I}s_j\operatorname{sym}(b_j,e_j).
\tag{3.8}
\]

For additional explicit coefficient checks, put `h_n=0`. The diagonal
and auxiliary cross coefficients are

\[
 P_{ii}=\operatorname{in}_i+s_i-h_i\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\mu_{ij}
       \quad(i<n),\qquad P_{nn}=1+\operatorname{in}_n+s_n,
\tag{3.9}
\]

\[
 P_{pp}=M(\alpha+\operatorname{out}_p),\qquad
 2P_{ip}=-M\mu_{ip}-\mu_{pi}
          -h_i\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\mu_{pj}\quad(0\le i\le n).
\tag{3.10}
\]

For `0<=i<j<=n`, the physical cross coefficient is

\[
 \begin{split}
 2P_{ij}={}&2h_i\mathbf1_{j=n}+h_i(\operatorname{in}_j+s_j)
 -\mu_{ji}-\mu_{ij}\\
 &-h_j\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{n}\mu_{i\ell}
 -h_i\sum_{\ell=i+1}^{n}\mu_{j\ell}.
 \end{split}
\tag{3.11}
\]

Equations (3.8)--(3.11) retain every original gradient coordinate. In
particular, (3.10) follows from the p balance in (3.5), not from absorption.

At a common nonstar gradient `G_i=v`, internal row terms vanish and

\[
 R_\alpha(v)=\left[-\eta M+\sum_j s_jt_j\right]\|v\|^2.
\tag{3.12}
\]

PSD requires this scalar to be nonnegative. It need not be zero, so there
is no general first-variation contact equation or common-translation
invariance.

One valid auxiliary reduction, if desired, follows directly from (3.10).
Set `A_p=M(alpha+out_p)>0` and

\[
 k_i=M\mu_{ip}+\mu_{pi}+h_i\sum_{j>i}\mu_{pj},\qquad
 z=\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{n}k_i g_i}{2A_p}.
\]

Then `R_alpha=A_p||p-z||^2+K(G_0,...,G_n)` by exact completion of
the p square. Moreover `k_i>=0` and

\[
 2A_p-\sum_i k_i=M s_p+\sum_{j=0}^{n}\mu_{pj}(M-t_j)\ge0,
\tag{3.13}
\]

so z is a subprobability mixture of the physical gradients, with its
remaining weight at zero. This is not p absorption and does not permit
setting `g_n=0` by a quotient. The exclusions below test literal principal
blocks of (3.8), without using this reduction.

## 4. One mechanism: dense regenerative routing

Define the physical probability profile

\[
 \pi_i=h_i/M\ (i<n),\qquad \pi_n=(S+1)/M,
 \qquad \sum_{i=0}^{n}\pi_i=1.
\tag{4.1}
\]

Choose a physical destination fraction `0<theta<1`, and set

\[
 q_i=\theta\pi_i\ (i\le n),\quad q_p=1-\theta,
 \quad \kappa=2+\eta=33/16,
 \quad L=\frac{2\kappa}{q_p}.
\tag{4.2}
\]

From (4.2) through Section 6, the letter L denotes a routing intensity.
The objective's smoothness parameter is normalized to one throughout
these sections; no physical step is rescaled by the routing intensity.

Define intensities

\[
 r_i=Lq_i+\kappa\mathbf1_{i=n}-\kappa\mathbf1_{i=p}.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Every original coefficient of the two laws is the following:

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \mu_{ij}=r_iq_j,&i,j\in I,\ i\ne j,\\
 s_n=\eta,\quad s_p=\eta,&s_i=0\ (i<n),\\
 \lambda_{ij}=\mu_{ij},&j\text{ physical},\\
 \lambda_{i,p}=M\mu_{i,p},&i\ne p,\\
 \lambda_{*,n}=\eta,\quad\lambda_{*,p}=M\eta,&
 \lambda_{*,i}=0\ (i<n).
 \end{array}
\tag{4.4}
\]

Self rows and destination-star rows are zero. Thus all unspecified
coefficients are also zero. The distinction between a routing intensity
and actual outgoing mass matters: `out_i=r_i(1-q_i)`, not `r_i`.

Because `sum q_i=1`, `sum r_i=L`, and `r_p=kappa>0`, all coefficients
are nonnegative and every p-to-physical row is strictly positive for the
actual strictly positive word. Directly,

\[
 \operatorname{out}_i-\operatorname{in}_i
 =r_i-Lq_i=\kappa\mathbf1_{i=n}-\kappa\mathbf1_{i=p}.
\tag{4.5}
\]

Subtracting the two star inputs gives divergence two at n, minus
`2 alpha` at p, and zero elsewhere. Hence the complete x0 cancellation
holds exactly. The star uses its entire `1/8` source budget.

Its common-gradient value is

\[
 R_\alpha(v)=\eta t_n\|v\|^2>0\qquad(v\ne0).
\tag{4.6}
\]

These laws therefore do not secretly reimpose the exact-U null condition.
Both physical-to-auxiliary and auxiliary-to-physical rows are present.

The two choices of theta below are the only laws tested. A useful robustness
observation is that (4.3)--(4.5) remain valid for any routing intensity
`L>=kappa/q_p`; the explicit laws use `2 kappa/q_p`. When analyzing the
second law's obstruction, allowing the larger interval of intensities is
an exclusion of possible repairs, not a third law.

## 5. Law 1: balanced destinations

Set `theta=1/2`, so `L=4 kappa=33/4`. This specifies every coefficient
of Law 1 via (4.4).

For a physical preterminal index i, let
`Q_i^+=sum_{j=i+1}^n q_j`. Formula (3.9) gives the exact literal diagonal

\[
 P_{ii}=Lq_i(1-q_i-h_iQ_i^+),\qquad
 q_i+h_iQ_i^+=\frac{\theta h_i(M+1-T_{i+1})}{M}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

No other coordinate has been eliminated. Set just `g_i=v`, with all other
gradients, including `p` and `g_n`, zero; (5.1) is the entire polynomial
on that formal assignment.

For the same-enclosing-m H3 prefix, write

\[
 C=1+S_2=4+2a+\beta,\qquad k=ac^2=\beta c,
 \qquad h_3=1+k,\quad T_4=C+k.
\tag{5.2}
\]

Its mass parameter is `M_3=4C+2k-1`. At `M=M_3`, strict negativity in
(5.1) is equivalent to

\[
 (1+k)(3C+k)>2(4C+2k-1).
\tag{5.3}
\]

The difference between the two sides is exactly

\[
 C(3k-5)+(k-1)(k-2)>0.
\tag{5.4}
\]

For its sign, `epsilon<1/10` and `1+sqrt(2)>12/5` give
`k=(1+sqrt(2))(1-epsilon^2)>297/125>7/3`. Both terms in
(5.4) are therefore strictly positive.

The original complete word H_m contains this identical H3 prefix and has
`M>=M_3`. Since `T_4>1`, the factor
`(M+1-T_4)/M=1-(T_4-1)/M` increases with M. Thus the same original
diagonal remains strictly negative for the complete word, for every
enclosing `m>=1536`. This is a full-space obstruction to Law 1, at one
specified original coordinate; no quotient or numerical calibration is
used.

## 6. Law 2: destinations damped by the largest step

Law 1 spends too much destination mass after a long step. For the second
and final law, let

\[
 d=\max_{i<n}h_i=1+ac^{m-1},\qquad
 \theta=\frac1{2d},\quad q_p=1-\frac1{2d},\quad
 L=\frac{2\kappa}{q_p}.
\tag{6.1}
\]

Again (4.4) specifies every coefficient. For the actual H_m, `d>3`.
The proof below works for every positive word having a step `d>=3` and
using this same profile and source split.

This adjustment repairs all coordinate diagonals. Indeed, for `i<n`,

\[
 q_i+h_iQ_i^+\le\theta+d\theta
 =\theta+\frac12\le\frac23,
\]

so `P_ii>=L q_i/3>0`. Also

\[
 P_{nn}=\alpha+(L-\kappa)q_n-Lq_n^2\ge\alpha>0,
\tag{6.2}
\]

because `L(1-q_n)>=Lq_p>=kappa`. Formula (3.10) gives a positive p
diagonal. These statements continue to hold if the routing intensity is
any `L>=kappa/q_p`.

Nevertheless the complete matrix fails on a two-coordinate original
principal block. Let i be an index carrying the largest step d, keep only
`g_i` and `g_n` free, and set every other coordinate, including p, zero.
Denote the two diagonal coefficients by `D_i=P_ii`, `D_n=P_nn`, and the
coefficient of `<g_i,g_n>` by C. This use of zero coordinates is a
particular formal test assignment, not an asserted quotient identity.

Put `q_+=sum_{j=i+1}^n q_j` and `y=Lq_n>0`. The exact original-row
formulas (3.9) and (3.11) give

\[
 \begin{split}
 D_i&=Lq_i(1-q_i-dq_+),\\
 D_n&=\alpha+(L-\kappa)q_n-Lq_n^2,\\
 C&=d(Lq_n+\kappa)(1-q_+)-(2Lq_n+\kappa)q_i.
 \end{split}
\tag{6.3}
\]

These formulas include the nonzero star-to-terminal row. In particular,
omitting it would incorrectly replace alpha in D_n and kappa in C.

Now

\[
 q_i=\frac1{2M},\qquad
 q_n=\frac{M+1}{4dM},\qquad q_+\le\frac1{2d}.
\tag{6.4}
\]

Since `S>=d`, we have `M>=2d+1`. Using (6.3)--(6.4),

\[
 D_iD_n\le\frac{L}{2M}(\alpha+y)
 =\frac{2d}{M+1}y(\alpha+y)<y(\alpha+y).
\tag{6.5}
\]

The cross coefficient has the lower bound

\[
 \begin{split}
 C&\ge\left(d-\frac12-\frac1M\right)y
       +\kappa\left(d-\frac12-\frac1{2M}\right)\\
  &\ge(d-1)(y+\kappa)>0.
 \end{split}
\tag{6.6}
\]

Because `d>=3`, (6.6) implies `C^2/4>=(y+kappa)^2`. But

\[
 (y+\kappa)^2-y(y+\alpha)
 =(2\kappa-\alpha)y+\kappa^2>0.
\tag{6.7}
\]

Consequently the actual principal determinant is strictly negative:

\[
 \boxed{D_iD_n-C^2/4<0.}
\tag{6.8}
\]

An explicit full formal negative direction is `g_i=v`,
`g_n=-C v/(2D_n)`, with every other coordinate zero. Its polynomial
value is `(D_i-C^2/(4D_n))||v||^2<0`. This keeps the terminal gradient
nonzero and tests the full unreduced polynomial. It is not asserted to
satisfy the projection normals.

The derivation of (6.8) holds for every `L>=kappa/q_p`, not only the
particular intensity in (6.1). Hence increasing the circular traffic,
including the auxiliary return flow, cannot repair this law. It fails
despite the correct source budget, exact divergence, nonnegative original
coefficients, positive common-gradient value, and positive coordinate
diagonals.

## 7. Exact full-W implication of the still-missing U bound

For completeness, the sufficient target (2.1) must use the entire polar
minimum set, not an origin-radius surrogate. For any allowed D, set

\[
 C_D=\{z:\langle z,g\rangle\le0\text{ for all }g\in D\}.
\]

This is exactly `argmin F_D`, with minimum zero. Membership makes every
candidate in F_D nonpositive, while `g=0` attains zero. Conversely, a
positive `<z,g>` produces a positive value using a sufficiently small
multiple of g in D. The set is closed, convex and nonempty.

For every selected minimum `z in C_D`, every g in D, and M>0,

\[
 2\langle x,g\rangle-M\|g\|^2
 \le 2\langle x-z,g\rangle-M\|g\|^2
 \le\frac{\|x-z\|^2}{M}.
\]

Thus `U_M(x)<=dist(x,C_D)^2/M<=||x-z||^2/M`. Since `U_1=2F_D`, an
all-body proof of (2.1) for each sufficiently large m would imply

\[
 2F_D(T_{H_m}x)\le\frac{17}{16M}\|x-z\|^2
\]

for every selected minimum and start. Starts in the minimum set are
stationary and have zero value. Accepted finite-word universality then
gives, for each fixed m separately,

\[
 \mathcal W_{2^m-1}(H_m)\le\frac{17}{16M}.
\tag{7.1}
\]

There is no horizon/supremum interchange. Multiplication of F_D by the
advertised smoothness and use of steps h/L preserves the original
normalization. Accepted `B_(2^m-1)/M<47/50` would then yield

\[
 \frac{\mathcal W_{2^m-1}^\star}{U_{2^m-1}^{\rm ZJ}}
 \le B_{2^m-1}\mathcal W_{2^m-1}(H_m)<\frac{799}{800}.
\]

It is enough that (7.1) hold eventually along these horizons to deduce
`liminf W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1`. The missing hypothesis remains the
uniform all-body, all-dimension, all-start upper bound (2.1). Neither
coefficient exclusion proves or disproves it.

## 8. Remaining gap and resource closeout

The exclusions concern the explicitly specified global common-destination
routing profile, at its two prescribed physical mass fractions and its
fixed equal star split between n and p. They do not exclude general dense
original-row flows, other destination profiles, other star allocations,
additional auxiliary projections, or the relaxed U inequality itself.
The second exclusion specifically identifies a mixed original-gradient
obstruction that survives all circulating-flow increases; checking only
diagonals would have missed it.

No all-depth PSD law was constructed. Replacing the remaining construction
by feasibility of (3.8) would be an equivalent certificate search, not a
solution; this invocation makes no such progress claim. A subsequent
authorized route would need a new coefficient mechanism or independent
mathematical information, rather than another circulation intensity in
these two laws.

Exactly one analytical invocation and exactly two laws were used. There
were zero mathematical programs, arithmetic/symbolic/trajectory/recurrence
replays, numerical-array loads, solvers, optimizers, formal calls,
fact submissions, extra models/agents, consults, installations,
worker/service changes, truth-store writes, publication, finalization,
revocation or shutdown. Only text reads, analytical reasoning, Markdown
persistence, and byte hashing occurred. Broad combined source reads were
truncated; the decisive accepted statements, actual H3 derivation, and
prior two-law outcome/scope were subsequently read in bounded text chunks.

The TASK SHA-256 was checked and equals
`2658f688be00c1ca6556dd38a875b8eb74cd4c11682cebaa80fd05c9f0eab634`.
The local memory registry was consulted only for project scope and evidence
boundaries; current authorization was taken from the reread OPERATOR and
this fresh TASK, not its historical pause snapshot.

All new derivations remain JOINT UNVERIFIED pending selective independent
review. Only this new output directory was written. Parent closes the
ledger. STOP at this bounded invocation; no automatic renewal. Original
mathematical target OPEN.

# Relaxed U propagation with genuine star-source freedom

NEW gpt-6-astra/ultra analytical invocation under the active persistent Goal. Read current OPERATOR.md and project PROBLEM.md. Original full asymptotic minimax target remains OPEN. User permits autonomous bounded calls, direct Codex reasoning and up to four concurrent agents. ConsultOFF.
Write only in D:/Danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/relaxed_u_star_search_20261008_r3. Read public shared facts/research, never worker local/private memory or conversations.

## Target and accepted inputs

W_n(h)=sup2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x0-xstar||^2) over ALL finite-dimensional convex L-smooth functions with an attained minimum and all selected minima/starts, using the original predetermined nonnegative h. W=2R.
For SAME enclosingm>=1536: eps1/(4m),beta1-eps,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps),a=beta/c,H0empty,H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),S_j=sumH_j,n=2^m-1,M=1+2S_m.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/M<47/50. Full W_n(H_m)<=17/(16M) eventually would disprove the original profile match. This upper is UNPROVED.
Read accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 for finite-word projection-simplex universality and the exact actualH3 U law.

For any compact convex D containing0, F_D(x)=max_(g inD)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),gradF_D=P_D. Define U_d(x)=max_(g inD)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2). The target of this call is

    U1(T_Hm x)<=alpha U_M(x), alpha=17/16,

uniformly in all bodies,dimensions,starts and sufficiently largem. Establish the original full-W implication using the entire polar minimum set; do not substitute a single body or finite depth.

## Different coefficient space, not an exact-U retry

Read main_relaxed_u_interface_lead_20261008_r2.md as an UNVERIFIED lead, and the exact-U source projection_flow_search_20261008_r2/REPORT.md for conventions only. Independently derive every new identity.
Use physicalnodes0..n,auxiliaryp=P_D(x0/M),zero star. Q_ij=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0. For nonstar destinations put mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij,withphysicala_j1,a_p1/M. Keep star->j and p->physical rows. Destination-star rows are identicallyzero.

Proposed x0 cancellation:
div_i(mu)=2(1_i=n-alpha*1_i=p) for nonstar i,
div_star=2(alpha-1)=1/8.
Proposed value-free full gradient polynomial:
R_alpha=2<g_n,sumh_i g_i>+||g_n||^2-alpha M||p||^2
 +sum_(nonstar i,j)mu_ij<B_jG,G_j-G_i>
 +sum_(nonstar j)mu_(star,j)<B_jG,G_j>,
B_iG=g_i+sum_(k<i)h_kg_k,B_pG=M p.

At common nonstar gradients this is
[(1-alpha)M+sum_j mu_(star,j)t_j]||g||^2,t_i1+prefixmass,t_pM.
This need not vanish. Do NOT impose old clock-contact equations, p absorption, or g_n=0 quotient as necessary. Retain the FULL original gradient matrix unless a new valid reduction is proved.

## One search mechanism, at most two laws

Construct a genuinely new explicit star-source/flow/energy law using the available fixed source budget1/8. It must specify every original coefficient, prove nonnegativity, and bound the complete matrix on the actual hierarchy at all sufficiently large depths.
At most TWO concrete laws within this mechanism. Use analytic sign calibrations when useful, but no numerical scans. Merely deriving an equivalent SDP or postponing all-depth PSD into a new theorem-strength hypothesis is BLOCKED, not progress.
The complete physical graph had positive finite exact-U candidate margins atH3/H4 (read only REPORT of projection_absorption_exactfixture_20261008_r2 for evidence); no exact coefficients were reconstructed. Do not treat floating candidates as premises.
The two joint-reserve gluing laws of projection_joint_reserve_search_20261008_r2 failed in stated H3 profile neighborhoods. Read their outcome/scope to avoid repeating absorption-product or moment-dual laws. The tree and monotoneA/D exclusions concern specified exact-U/diagonalized classes and do not automatically transfer to this relaxed class.

Prefer a directly quantitative17/16 argument over proving stronger exact-U/all-d properties. If a law fails, give its first exact original-row/coefficient obstruction and scope. A formal negative direction is not a legal GD counterexample. If an actual counterexample is found, verify its fixed function,full trajectory,minimum and original objective before claiming it.

## Resources/output

ZERO mathematical programs, arithmetic/symbolic/trajectory/recurrence replay, numerical arrays/solvers/optimizers, formal calls/fact_submit, consults, extra models/agents, installations, worker/service changes, truth-store writes, publication,finalization,revocation or shutdown.

# R6 handoff

UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN. No complete affirmative or negative candidate.

One invocation, two substantive lines, mathematical PROGRAM0, arrays/solvers0, formal0, no additional agent/model. Only the two requested R6 output files were written. Parent owns ledger and closeout.

## Decisive result

Common-normal translation is valid, but its exact changes are

\[
H\mapsto H+t,\quad M\mapsto M+t,\quad F_n\mapsto F_n+t,\quad
R^2\mapsto R^2+2t\langle x_0,e\rangle+t^2\|e\|^2.
\]

If the minimum-norm affine foot belongs to the retained nonzero gradient hull, orthogonal projection off that common component produces another actual static projection-gradient function with the same complete physical word. With tangent radius \(D\), tangent terminal value \(V=2F_T\), common gradient component \(c\), and initial normal coordinate \(y\), the full error is

\[
\frac{V+2cy-(2S+1)c^2}{D+y^2}
\le\max\{V/D,\ 1/(2S+1)\}.
\]

This is an exact, whole-word domination statement. The tangent history is explicitly realized, not a formal Gram direction.

For the R5 orthogonal retained-height construction, the foot condition holds and every legal translation leaves the initial point in the gradient cone. Therefore 0 is genuinely the closest minimizer; no polar-cone improvement was omitted. Its tangent generators form a centered simplex after scaling.

The second line explicitly restores a common normal to the R5 centered simplex with a shared gradient component and negative cross correlations. Every old and minimum row is checked. Its initial point is again in the gradient cone, and the same domination applies for every legal lift and positive replenishment parameter. Optimizing or repeating this repair cannot outperform its actual centered child or the Huber witness.

The foot condition is also automatic for linearly independent generators with nonpositive cross inner products, by positivity of the solution of their Gram system. This broader observation does not itself prove that 0 is closest for arbitrary histories; the report keeps that distinction explicit.

## Baseline-surviving word check

The parent's R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md was read. Its exact multiplicities, mass inductions, and three frequency cases were checked manually and support
\(D_m\sup_\lambda\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2\le9/16\).
Conditional on the parent-reported accepted comparison \(B/D_m<47/50\), the inherited-parameter hierarchy survives the complete Huber-plus-scalar-quadratic pool. This invocation did not independently re-read that accepted fact from the fact service.

On those words, a replenished witness can succeed only if its centered tangent history already supplies the missing target-level lower bound. No upper bound on all such tangent histories was obtained. Do not call this a full-family failure or a negative resolution.

## Remaining original-target work

There is still no proof selecting centered or otherwise compatible histories for every surviving word with a uniform \((1-o(1))/B_n\) payoff. The proposed common-normal replenishment does not fill that gap. Do not renew unchanged common shifts as though their height increase were free.

The R6 allocation is complete and stops here. No formal submission or automatic renewal is requested.
